My husband says they’re just being friendly, but it doesn’t feel friendly in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, taken out, or that others around you can hear what you’re thinking?\n\nPatient: Not really like that… I don’t think anyone is putting thoughts in my head or taking them out. I don’t think so… not in that way. Like they ask too many questions about the new showroom, and then I start thinking they’re checking whether I’m going to miss payments or something. My husband says they’re just being friendly, you know, people like to ask about renovations and display cases and all that, but it doesn’t feel that way to me when they keep asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else, taken out of your mind, or that others can hear your thoughts?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like someone putting thoughts in or taking them out, no… I don’t think so. Like with the new showroom, they keep asking questions, and I start thinking they’re checking whether I’m going to miss payments or something. My husband says they’re just being friendly, but it doesn’t feel friendly in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, taken out, or that others around you can hear what you’re thinking?\n\nPatient: Not really like that… I don’t think anyone is putting thoughts in my head or taking them out. And I know my husband says they’re just being friendly, people ask about renovations, but it starts feeling like there’s another reason behind it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by an outside force, like someone is putting thoughts in your head? Like at the shop, someone will ask me a simple thing—when their ring repair will be ready—and I start answering, but then I’m talking about invoices, and then somehow garden soil, and then mortgage papers… and I can see their face change, like I’ve lost them. It’s embarrassing, and I don’t always know how I got there.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any unusual thoughts or beliefs lately that others might not understand or seem strange to you?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes, a little. I know it probably sounds odd, but I’ve been getting really convinced that some customers aren’t just coming in for jewelry. Like they ask too many questions about the new showroom, and then I start thinking they’re checking whether I’m going to miss payments or something. My husband says they’re just being friendly, you know, people like to ask about renovations and display cases and all that, but it doesn’t feel that way to me when they keep asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else, taken out of your mind, or that others can hear your thoughts?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like someone putting thoughts in or taking them out, no… I don’t think so. It’s more that I get stuck on the idea that people in the shop are there for some reason they’re not saying. Like with the new showroom, they keep asking questions, and I start thinking they’re checking whether I’m going to miss payments or something. My husband says they’re just being friendly, but it doesn’t feel friendly in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, taken out, or that others around you can hear what you’re thinking?\n\nPatient: Not really like that… I don’t think anyone is putting thoughts in my head or taking them out. It’s more that I get this idea and I can’t shake it—like some customers are coming in to check on me, to see if I’m going to miss payments, because they keep asking about the new showroom. And I know my husband says they’re just being friendly, people ask about renovations, but it starts feeling like there’s another reason behind it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by an outside force, like someone is putting thoughts in your head? Or that your thoughts are being removed, or that others can hear your thoughts as you think them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in that way. I mean, I get muddled sometimes and I overthink things, especially when people are asking me too many questions, but I wouldn’t say it feels like someone is putting thoughts into my head or pulling them out. And I don’t really think people can hear what I’m thinking… though sometimes I do wonder if I’m giving something away with my face. You know, my mother used to say I had “no poker face,” which is funny because in jewelry you really do need one sometimes. But no, not like my thoughts are being broadcast or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had the experience of knowing, without the use of your usual senses, that something is happening or that someone is thinking something? For example, have you ever felt that someone else could know your thoughts, or that your thoughts were being taken out of your head or put in by someone else?\n\nPatient: Not like… hearing someone’s thoughts, no. I don’t think I can know things like that. But I do get convinced sometimes that people are coming into the shop for a reason they’re not saying. Like with the new showroom—customers ask all these questions, and after a while it feels like they’re checking whether I’m going to miss payments or whether something’s wrong. My husband says they’re just being friendly, and maybe they are, but in the moment I can’t shake it. It sounds odd when I say it out loud.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you—like something on TV, a song playing, or something someone says—have a special meaning that’s directed at you personally?\n\nPatient: Not really with TV or songs, I don’t think. It’s more… things people say in the shop. Like when customers keep asking about the new showroom—how it’s coming along, when it’ll be finished, all these little questions—and I start feeling like it means something more, like they’re checking whether I’m going to miss payments. My husband says they’re just being friendly, you know, people like to talk about renovations… and I know that sounds reasonable, but sometimes I get stuck on it and I can’t shake it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had the feeling that your thoughts are not your own, that someone else is responsible for them, or that you are losing control over your thoughts in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t feel like someone else is putting them there, no… but I do feel like I lose the thread of them. That’s actually part of why I came in. Someone can ask me something simple at the shop, like when a ring repair will be ready, and I start answering, and suddenly I’m talking about invoices, then the soil I need for the garden, then mortgage papers, and I can see their face change like they don’t know how we got there. Then I get embarrassed and try to pull it back, but it feels like my thoughts kind of run off without me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, taken out, or that others around you can hear your thoughts as you think them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s like someone putting thoughts in my head, or people hearing them exactly. It’s more that I get stuck on an idea and it feels true at the time. Like with customers at the shop asking about the new showroom—too many questions, you know, when it’ll be done, how it’s going—and I start thinking they’re really there to see if I’m going to miss payments or if something’s wrong. My husband says they’re just being friendly, and maybe he’s right, but in the moment I get convinced. It sounds odd when I say it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Can you tell me what’s been bringing you in today?\n\nPatient: Well… it’s been hard to explain, but I came in because I’ve been getting sort of tangled when I talk. Like at the shop, someone will ask me a simple thing—when their ring repair will be ready—and I start answering, but then I’m talking about invoices, and then somehow garden soil, and then mortgage papers… and I can see their face change, like I’ve lost them. It’s embarrassing, and I don’t always know how I got there.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any unusual thoughts or beliefs lately that others might not understand or seem strange to you?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes, a little. I know it probably sounds odd, but I’ve been getting really convinced that some customers aren’t just coming in for jewelry. Like they ask too many questions about the new showroom, and then I start thinking they’re checking whether I’m going to miss payments or something. My husband says they’re just being friendly, you know, people like to ask about renovations and display cases and all that, but it doesn’t feel that way to me when they keep asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else, taken out of your mind, or that others can hear your thoughts?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like someone putting thoughts in or taking them out, no… I don’t think so. It’s more that I get stuck on the idea that people in the shop are there for some reason they’re not saying. Like with the new showroom, they keep asking questions, and I start thinking they’re checking whether I’m going to miss payments or something. My husband says they’re just being friendly, but it doesn’t feel friendly in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, taken out, or that others around you can hear what you’re thinking?\n\nPatient: Not really like that… I don’t think anyone is putting thoughts in my head or taking them out. It’s more that I get this idea and I can’t shake it—like some customers are coming in to check on me, to see if I’m going to miss payments, because they keep asking about the new showroom. And I know my husband says they’re just being friendly, people ask about renovations, but it starts feeling like there’s another reason behind it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by an outside force, like someone is putting thoughts in your head? Or that your thoughts are being removed, or that others can hear your thoughts as you think them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in that way. I mean, I get muddled sometimes and I overthink things, especially when people are asking me too many questions, but I wouldn’t say it feels like someone is putting thoughts into my head or pulling them out. And I don’t really think people can hear what I’m thinking… though sometimes I do wonder if I’m giving something away with my face. You know, my mother used to say I had “no poker face,” which is funny because in jewelry you really do need one sometimes. But no, not like my thoughts are being broadcast or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had the experience of knowing, without the use of your usual senses, that something is happening or that someone is thinking something? For example, have you ever felt that someone else could know your thoughts, or that your thoughts were being taken out of your head or put in by someone else?\n\nPatient: Not like… hearing someone’s thoughts, no. I don’t think I can know things like that. But I do get convinced sometimes that people are coming into the shop for a reason they’re not saying. Like with the new showroom—customers ask all these questions, and after a while it feels like they’re checking whether I’m going to miss payments or whether something’s wrong. My husband says they’re just being friendly, and maybe they are, but in the moment I can’t shake it. It sounds odd when I say it out loud.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you—like something on TV, a song playing, or something someone says—have a special meaning that’s directed at you personally?\n\nPatient: Not really with TV or songs, I don’t think. It’s more… things people say in the shop. Like when customers keep asking about the new showroom—how it’s coming along, when it’ll be finished, all these little questions—and I start feeling like it means something more, like they’re checking whether I’m going to miss payments. My husband says they’re just being friendly, you know, people like to talk about renovations… and I know that sounds reasonable, but sometimes I get stuck on it and I can’t shake it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had the feeling that your thoughts are not your own, that someone else is responsible for them, or that you are losing control over your thoughts in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t feel like someone else is putting them there, no… but I do feel like I lose the thread of them. That’s actually part of why I came in. Someone can ask me something simple at the shop, like when a ring repair will be ready, and I start answering, and suddenly I’m talking about invoices, then the soil I need for the garden, then mortgage papers, and I can see their face change like they don’t know how we got there. Then I get embarrassed and try to pull it back, but it feels like my thoughts kind of run off without me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, taken out, or that others around you can hear your thoughts as you think them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s like someone putting thoughts in my head, or people hearing them exactly. It’s more that I get stuck on an idea and it feels true at the time. Like with customers at the shop asking about the new showroom—too many questions, you know, when it’ll be done, how it’s going—and I start thinking they’re really there to see if I’m going to miss payments or if something’s wrong. My husband says they’re just being friendly, and maybe he’s right, but in the moment I get convinced. It sounds odd when I say it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, taken out of your mind, or that others around you can hear your thoughts as you think them?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. Not like someone is putting thoughts in, or taking them out, or hearing them. I mean, I can get muddled trying to explain it—like even now I’m thinking about whether I locked the display case before I left, which is silly—but that’s just me getting sidetracked. I haven’t noticed anything like my thoughts being controlled or broadcast.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It feels like I’m following it, sort of, while I’m saying it. Like there’s a connection in my head, but then when it comes out loud it’s… too many side roads. Then I realize I’ve gone too far off, but it’s hard to get back to the original question.

My husband says they’re just being friendly, you know, people like to talk about renovations… and I know that sounds reasonable, but sometimes I get stuck on it and I can’t shake it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had the feeling that your thoughts are not your own, that someone else is responsible for them, or that you are losing control over your thoughts in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t feel like someone else is putting them there, no… but I do feel like I lose the thread of them. That’s actually part of why I came in. Someone can ask me something simple at the shop, like when a ring repair will be ready, and I start answering, and suddenly I’m talking about invoices, then the soil I need for the garden, then mortgage papers, and I can see their face change like they don’t know how we got there. Then I get embarrassed and try to pull it back, but it feels like my thoughts kind of run off without me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, taken out, or that others around you can hear your thoughts as you think them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s like someone putting thoughts in my head, or people hearing them exactly. It’s more that I get stuck on an idea and it feels true at the time. Like with customers at the shop asking about the new showroom—too many questions, you know, when it’ll be done, how it’s going—and I start thinking they’re really there to see if I’m going to miss payments or if something’s wrong. My husband says they’re just being friendly, and maybe he’s right, but in the moment I get convinced. It sounds odd when I say it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, taken out of your mind, or that others around you can hear your thoughts as you think them?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. Not like someone is putting thoughts in, or taking them out, or hearing them. I mean, I can get muddled trying to explain it—like even now I’m thinking about whether I locked the display case before I left, which is silly—but that’s just me getting sidetracked. I haven’t noticed anything like my thoughts being controlled or broadcast.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe what it feels like when your thoughts seem to run off without you? Do they feel disconnected from each other, or do you feel like you're following a logical path even if others don't see the connection?\n\nPatient: It feels like I’m following it, sort of, while I’m saying it. Like there’s a connection in my head, but then when it comes out loud it’s… too many side roads. Someone asks me something simple, like when a ring repair will be ready, and I start to answer, but then I’m talking about the invoice for it, and that reminds me of the papers on my desk, and then somehow I’m thinking about garden soil I meant to buy, and then the mortgage papers, and by then I can see their face, like they’ve completely lost me. Then I realize I’ve gone too far off, but it’s hard to get back to the original question.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being put into your mind by an outside force, like someone is placing thoughts in your head, or that your thoughts are being taken out of your mind or that others can hear your thoughts as you're thinking them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think that’s happened, no. I know I keep circling around it, but it doesn’t feel like someone outside me is placing thoughts there or pulling them out. And I don’t have the sense that people can hear them while I’m thinking them.\n\nSometimes I lose my train of thought and then I’m thinking about three other things, like something at the shop or whether I left a drawer open, but that still feels like my own mind being busy, not like someone else is doing it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It’s embarrassing, and I don’t always know how I got there.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any unusual thoughts or beliefs lately that others might not understand or seem strange to you?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes, a little. I know it probably sounds odd, but I’ve been getting really convinced that some customers aren’t just coming in for jewelry. Like they ask too many questions about the new showroom, and then I start thinking they’re checking whether I’m going to miss payments or something. My husband says they’re just being friendly, you know, people like to ask about renovations and display cases and all that, but it doesn’t feel that way to me when they keep asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else, taken out of your mind, or that others can hear your thoughts?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like someone putting thoughts in or taking them out, no… I don’t think so. It’s more that I get stuck on the idea that people in the shop are there for some reason they’re not saying. Like with the new showroom, they keep asking questions, and I start thinking they’re checking whether I’m going to miss payments or something. My husband says they’re just being friendly, but it doesn’t feel friendly in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, taken out, or that others around you can hear what you’re thinking?\n\nPatient: Not really like that… I don’t think anyone is putting thoughts in my head or taking them out. It’s more that I get this idea and I can’t shake it—like some customers are coming in to check on me, to see if I’m going to miss payments, because they keep asking about the new showroom. And I know my husband says they’re just being friendly, people ask about renovations, but it starts feeling like there’s another reason behind it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by an outside force, like someone is putting thoughts in your head? Or that your thoughts are being removed, or that others can hear your thoughts as you think them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in that way. I mean, I get muddled sometimes and I overthink things, especially when people are asking me too many questions, but I wouldn’t say it feels like someone is putting thoughts into my head or pulling them out. And I don’t really think people can hear what I’m thinking… though sometimes I do wonder if I’m giving something away with my face. You know, my mother used to say I had “no poker face,” which is funny because in jewelry you really do need one sometimes. But no, not like my thoughts are being broadcast or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had the experience of knowing, without the use of your usual senses, that something is happening or that someone is thinking something? For example, have you ever felt that someone else could know your thoughts, or that your thoughts were being taken out of your head or put in by someone else?\n\nPatient: Not like… hearing someone’s thoughts, no. I don’t think I can know things like that. But I do get convinced sometimes that people are coming into the shop for a reason they’re not saying. Like with the new showroom—customers ask all these questions, and after a while it feels like they’re checking whether I’m going to miss payments or whether something’s wrong. My husband says they’re just being friendly, and maybe they are, but in the moment I can’t shake it. It sounds odd when I say it out loud.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you—like something on TV, a song playing, or something someone says—have a special meaning that’s directed at you personally?\n\nPatient: Not really with TV or songs, I don’t think. It’s more… things people say in the shop. Like when customers keep asking about the new showroom—how it’s coming along, when it’ll be finished, all these little questions—and I start feeling like it means something more, like they’re checking whether I’m going to miss payments. My husband says they’re just being friendly, you know, people like to talk about renovations… and I know that sounds reasonable, but sometimes I get stuck on it and I can’t shake it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had the feeling that your thoughts are not your own, that someone else is responsible for them, or that you are losing control over your thoughts in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t feel like someone else is putting them there, no… but I do feel like I lose the thread of them. That’s actually part of why I came in. Someone can ask me something simple at the shop, like when a ring repair will be ready, and I start answering, and suddenly I’m talking about invoices, then the soil I need for the garden, then mortgage papers, and I can see their face change like they don’t know how we got there. Then I get embarrassed and try to pull it back, but it feels like my thoughts kind of run off without me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, taken out, or that others around you can hear your thoughts as you think them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s like someone putting thoughts in my head, or people hearing them exactly. It’s more that I get stuck on an idea and it feels true at the time. Like with customers at the shop asking about the new showroom—too many questions, you know, when it’ll be done, how it’s going—and I start thinking they’re really there to see if I’m going to miss payments or if something’s wrong. My husband says they’re just being friendly, and maybe he’s right, but in the moment I get convinced. It sounds odd when I say it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, taken out of your mind, or that others around you can hear your thoughts as you think them?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. Not like someone is putting thoughts in, or taking them out, or hearing them. I mean, I can get muddled trying to explain it—like even now I’m thinking about whether I locked the display case before I left, which is silly—but that’s just me getting sidetracked. I haven’t noticed anything like my thoughts being controlled or broadcast.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe what it feels like when your thoughts seem to run off without you? Do they feel disconnected from each other, or do you feel like you're following a logical path even if others don't see the connection?\n\nPatient: It feels like I’m following it, sort of, while I’m saying it. Like there’s a connection in my head, but then when it comes out loud it’s… too many side roads. Someone asks me something simple, like when a ring repair will be ready, and I start to answer, but then I’m talking about the invoice for it, and that reminds me of the papers on my desk, and then somehow I’m thinking about garden soil I meant to buy, and then the mortgage papers, and by then I can see their face, like they’ve completely lost me. Then I realize I’ve gone too far off, but it’s hard to get back to the original question.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being put into your mind by an outside force, like someone is placing thoughts in your head, or that your thoughts are being taken out of your mind or that others can hear your thoughts as you're thinking them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think that’s happened, no. I know I keep circling around it, but it doesn’t feel like someone outside me is placing thoughts there or pulling them out. And I don’t have the sense that people can hear them while I’m thinking them.\n\nSometimes I lose my train of thought and then I’m thinking about three other things, like something at the shop or whether I left a drawer open, but that still feels like my own mind being busy, not like someone else is doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had the experience where it felt like your thoughts were being put into your mind by someone or something else, taken out of your mind, or that others around you could hear your thoughts as you were thinking them?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think so. I mean, I’ve had times where my thoughts jump around and I’m not finishing one before another one comes in, like if I’m looking at a stone setting and then suddenly remembering something completely different I meant to do… but it doesn’t feel like someone else put it there.\n\nAnd I haven’t noticed thoughts being taken out, or people hearing them. If someone guesses what I’m thinking, I usually figure it’s because my face gave it away or I said half of it out loud without realizing. But no, not in the way you’re asking, I don’t think.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]

Like there’s a connection in my head, but then when it comes out loud it’s… too many side roads. Someone asks me something simple, like when a ring repair will be ready, and I start to answer, but then I’m talking about the invoice for it, and that reminds me of the papers on my desk, and then somehow I’m thinking about garden soil I meant to buy, and then the mortgage papers, and by then I can see their face, like they’ve completely lost me. Then I realize I’ve gone too far off, but it’s hard to get back to the original question.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being put into your mind by an outside force, like someone is placing thoughts in your head, or that your thoughts are being taken out of your mind or that others can hear your thoughts as you're thinking them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think that’s happened, no. I know I keep circling around it, but it doesn’t feel like someone outside me is placing thoughts there or pulling them out. And I don’t have the sense that people can hear them while I’m thinking them.\n\nSometimes I lose my train of thought and then I’m thinking about three other things, like something at the shop or whether I left a drawer open, but that still feels like my own mind being busy, not like someone else is doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had the experience where it felt like your thoughts were being put into your mind by someone or something else, taken out of your mind, or that others around you could hear your thoughts as you were thinking them?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think so. I mean, I’ve had times where my thoughts jump around and I’m not finishing one before another one comes in, like if I’m looking at a stone setting and then suddenly remembering something completely different I meant to do… but it doesn’t feel like someone else put it there.\n\nAnd I haven’t noticed thoughts being taken out, or people hearing them. If someone guesses what I’m thinking, I usually figure it’s because my face gave it away or I said half of it out loud without realizing. But no, not in the way you’re asking, I don’t think.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone or something else, like they’re not coming from you at all?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 53 / jeweler_or_precious_stone_or_metal_worker / some_college\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, I don’t think so. I mean, thoughts come in fast sometimes, like I’ll be thinking about one thing and then another thing just barges in, but it still feels like it’s from me. Not like someone planted it there.

It’s more like when I’m sorting stones and I mean to stay with one tray, and then I notice a different setting that needs cleaning, and then I’m halfway across the bench before I remember what I started doing. But that’s me getting pulled off track, not something outside putting thoughts in my head.

Like they ask too many questions about the new showroom, and then I start thinking they’re checking whether I’m going to miss payments or something. My husband says they’re just being friendly, you know, people like to ask about renovations and display cases and all that, but it doesn’t feel that way to me when they keep asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else, taken out of your mind, or that others can hear your thoughts?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like someone putting thoughts in or taking them out, no… I don’t think so. My husband says they’re just being friendly, but it doesn’t feel friendly in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, taken out, or that others around you can hear what you’re thinking?\n\nPatient: Not really like that… I don’t think anyone is putting thoughts in my head or taking them out. You know, my mother used to say I had “no poker face,” which is funny because in jewelry you really do need one sometimes. My husband says they’re just being friendly, and maybe they are, but in the moment I can’t shake it. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… sometimes. I know it probably sounds strange, but at the shop I’ve gotten it in my head that certain customers aren’t really there just to look at jewelry.

Like they’ll ask a lot of questions about the new showroom, and I start thinking they’re checking whether I’m keeping up with payments or if I’m going to slip up somehow. My husband says they’re just being friendly, or curious because the place looks different now, and maybe that’s true. But when they keep asking, it feels pointed at me, like there’s a reason behind it.

And then I’ll start watching how they look at the cases, or if they glance at the counter too long… I don’t know. It’s not every customer, but with some of them it feels like it means something.

    "role": "system",
    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F21, F20.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what’s been bringing you in today?\n\nPatient: Well… it’s been hard to explain, but I came in because I’ve been getting sort of tangled when I talk. Like at the shop, someone will ask me a simple thing—when their ring repair will be ready—and I start answering, but then I’m talking about invoices, and then somehow garden soil, and then mortgage papers… and I can see their face change, like I’ve lost them. It’s embarrassing, and I don’t always know how I got there.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any unusual thoughts or beliefs lately that others might not understand or seem strange to you?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes, a little. I know it probably sounds odd, but I’ve been getting really convinced that some customers aren’t just coming in for jewelry. Like they ask too many questions about the new showroom, and then I start thinking they’re checking whether I’m going to miss payments or something. My husband says they’re just being friendly, you know, people like to ask about renovations and display cases and all that, but it doesn’t feel that way to me when they keep asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else, taken out of your mind, or that others can hear your thoughts?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like someone putting thoughts in or taking them out, no… I don’t think so. It’s more that I get stuck on the idea that people in the shop are there for some reason they’re not saying. Like with the new showroom, they keep asking questions, and I start thinking they’re checking whether I’m going to miss payments or something. My husband says they’re just being friendly, but it doesn’t feel friendly in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, taken out, or that others around you can hear what you’re thinking?\n\nPatient: Not really like that… I don’t think anyone is putting thoughts in my head or taking them out. It’s more that I get this idea and I can’t shake it—like some customers are coming in to check on me, to see if I’m going to miss payments, because they keep asking about the new showroom. And I know my husband says they’re just being friendly, people ask about renovations, but it starts feeling like there’s another reason behind it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by an outside force, like someone is putting thoughts in your head? Or that your thoughts are being removed, or that others can hear your thoughts as you think them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in that way. I mean, I get muddled sometimes and I overthink things, especially when people are asking me too many questions, but I wouldn’t say it feels like someone is putting thoughts into my head or pulling them out. And I don’t really think people can hear what I’m thinking… though sometimes I do wonder if I’m giving something away with my face. You know, my mother used to say I had “no poker face,” which is funny because in jewelry you really do need one sometimes. But no, not like my thoughts are being broadcast or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had the experience of knowing, without the use of your usual senses, that something is happening or that someone is thinking something? For example, have you ever felt that someone else could know your thoughts, or that your thoughts were being taken out of your head or put in by someone else?\n\nPatient: Not like… hearing someone’s thoughts, no. I don’t think I can know things like that. But I do get convinced sometimes that people are coming into the shop for a reason they’re not saying. Like with the new showroom—customers ask all these questions, and after a while it feels like they’re checking whether I’m going to miss payments or whether something’s wrong. My husband says they’re just being friendly, and maybe they are, but in the moment I can’t shake it. It sounds odd when I say it out loud.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you—like something on TV, a song playing, or something someone says—have a special meaning that’s directed at you personally?\n\nPatient: Not really with TV or songs, I don’t think. It’s more… things people say in the shop. Like when customers keep asking about the new showroom—how it’s coming along, when it’ll be finished, all these little questions—and I start feeling like it means something more, like they’re checking whether I’m going to miss payments. My husband says they’re just being friendly, you know, people like to talk about renovations… and I know that sounds reasonable, but sometimes I get stuck on it and I can’t shake it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had the feeling that your thoughts are not your own, that someone else is responsible for them, or that you are losing control over your thoughts in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t feel like someone else is putting them there, no… but I do feel like I lose the thread of them. That’s actually part of why I came in. Someone can ask me something simple at the shop, like when a ring repair will be ready, and I start answering, and suddenly I’m talking about invoices, then the soil I need for the garden, then mortgage papers, and I can see their face change like they don’t know how we got there. Then I get embarrassed and try to pull it back, but it feels like my thoughts kind of run off without me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, taken out, or that others around you can hear your thoughts as you think them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s like someone putting thoughts in my head, or people hearing them exactly. It’s more that I get stuck on an idea and it feels true at the time. Like with customers at the shop asking about the new showroom—too many questions, you know, when it’ll be done, how it’s going—and I start thinking they’re really there to see if I’m going to miss payments or if something’s wrong. My husband says they’re just being friendly, and maybe he’s right, but in the moment I get convinced. It sounds odd when I say it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, taken out of your mind, or that others around you can hear your thoughts as you think them?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. Not like someone is putting thoughts in, or taking them out, or hearing them. I mean, I can get muddled trying to explain it—like even now I’m thinking about whether I locked the display case before I left, which is silly—but that’s just me getting sidetracked. I haven’t noticed anything like my thoughts being controlled or broadcast.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe what it feels like when your thoughts seem to run off without you? Do they feel disconnected from each other, or do you feel like you're following a logical path even if others don't see the connection?\n\nPatient: It feels like I’m following it, sort of, while I’m saying it. Like there’s a connection in my head, but then when it comes out loud it’s… too many side roads. Someone asks me something simple, like when a ring repair will be ready, and I start to answer, but then I’m talking about the invoice for it, and that reminds me of the papers on my desk, and then somehow I’m thinking about garden soil I meant to buy, and then the mortgage papers, and by then I can see their face, like they’ve completely lost me. Then I realize I’ve gone too far off, but it’s hard to get back to the original question.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being put into your mind by an outside force, like someone is placing thoughts in your head, or that your thoughts are being taken out of your mind or that others can hear your thoughts as you're thinking them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think that’s happened, no. I know I keep circling around it, but it doesn’t feel like someone outside me is placing thoughts there or pulling them out. I mean, I’ve had times where my thoughts jump around and I’m not finishing one before another one comes in, like if I’m looking at a stone setting and then suddenly remembering something completely different I meant to do… but it doesn’t feel like someone else put it there.\n\nAnd I haven’t noticed thoughts being taken out, or people hearing them. Do they feel disconnected from each other, or do you feel like you're following a logical path even if others don't see the connection?\n\nPatient: It feels like I’m following it, sort of, while I’m saying it. Like there’s a connection in my head, but then when it comes out loud it’s… too many side roads. Someone asks me something simple, like when a ring repair will be ready, and I start to answer, but then I’m talking about the invoice for it, and that reminds me of the papers on my desk, and then somehow I’m thinking about garden soil I meant to buy, and then the mortgage papers, and by then I can see their face, like they’ve completely lost me. Then I realize I’ve gone too far off, but it’s hard to get back to the original question.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being put into your mind by an outside force, like someone is placing thoughts in your head, or that your thoughts are being taken out of your mind or that others can hear your thoughts as you're thinking them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think that’s happened, no. I know I keep circling around it, but it doesn’t feel like someone outside me is placing thoughts there or pulling them out. And I don’t have the sense that people can hear them while I’m thinking them.\n\nSometimes I lose my train of thought and then I’m thinking about three other things, like something at the shop or whether I left a drawer open, but that still feels like my own mind being busy, not like someone else is doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had the experience where it felt like your thoughts were being put into your mind by someone or something else, taken out of your mind, or that others around you could hear your thoughts as you were thinking them?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think so. I mean, I’ve had times where my thoughts jump around and I’m not finishing one before another one comes in, like if I’m looking at a stone setting and then suddenly remembering something completely different I meant to do… but it doesn’t feel like someone else put it there.\n\nAnd I haven’t noticed thoughts being taken out, or people hearing them. If someone guesses what I’m thinking, I usually figure it’s because my face gave it away or I said half of it out loud without realizing. But no, not in the way you’re asking, I don’t think.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone or something else, like they’re not coming from you at all?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, thoughts come in fast sometimes, like I’ll be thinking about one thing and then another thing just barges in, but it still feels like it’s from me. Not like someone planted it there.\n\nIt’s more like when I’m sorting stones and I mean to stay with one tray, and then I notice a different setting that needs cleaning, and then I’m halfway across the bench before I remember what I started doing. But that’s me getting pulled off track, not something outside putting thoughts in my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain events, actions, or objects in your environment were specifically directed at you or had a special meaning for you that others might not understand?\n\nPatient: Yes… sometimes. I know it probably sounds strange, but at the shop I’ve gotten it in my head that certain customers aren’t really there just to look at jewelry.\n\nLike they’ll ask a lot of questions about the new showroom, and I start thinking they’re checking whether I’m keeping up with payments or if I’m going to slip up somehow. My husband says they’re just being friendly, or curious because the place looks different now, and maybe that’s true. But when they keep asking, it feels pointed at me, like there’s a reason behind it.\n\nAnd then I’ll start watching how they look at the cases, or if they glance at the counter too long… I don’t know. It’s not every customer, but with some of them it feels like it means something.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Not really like thoughts being put in or taken out, no. I don’t feel like someone is reaching into my head that way.

It’s more that I get stuck on an idea and it feels true at the time. Like with customers at the shop asking about the new showroom—too many questions, you know, when it’ll be done, how it’s going—and I start thinking they’re really there to see if I’m going to miss payments or if something’s wrong. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not like… thoughts being put in or pulled out, no. I don’t think I’ve had that.

The closest thing is, well, I know it sounds odd, but at the showroom I’ve gotten really convinced some customers are coming in for a reason. They’ll ask too many questions about the new place, the cases, how business is going, and I start thinking they’re checking whether I’ll miss payments or mess something up. My husband says they’re just being friendly, and maybe they are, but it doesn’t feel that way to me when it’s happening.

    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. Can you tell me what’s been bringing you in today?\n\nPatient: Well… it’s been hard to explain, but I came in because I’ve been getting sort of tangled when I talk. Like at the shop, someone will ask me a simple thing—when their ring repair will be ready—and I start answering, but then I’m talking about invoices, and then somehow garden soil, and then mortgage papers… and I can see their face change, like I’ve lost them. It’s embarrassing, and I don’t always know how I got there.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any unusual thoughts or beliefs lately that others might not understand or seem strange to you?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes, a little. I know it probably sounds odd, but I’ve been getting really convinced that some customers aren’t just coming in for jewelry. Like they ask too many questions about the new showroom, and then I start thinking they’re checking whether I’m going to miss payments or something. My husband says they’re just being friendly, you know, people like to ask about renovations and display cases and all that, but it doesn’t feel that way to me when they keep asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else, taken out of your mind, or that others can hear your thoughts?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like someone putting thoughts in or taking them out, no… I don’t think so. It’s more that I get stuck on the idea that people in the shop are there for some reason they’re not saying. Like with the new showroom, they keep asking questions, and I start thinking they’re checking whether I’m going to miss payments or something. My husband says they’re just being friendly, but it doesn’t feel friendly in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, taken out, or that others around you can hear what you’re thinking?\n\nPatient: Not really like that… I don’t think anyone is putting thoughts in my head or taking them out. It’s more that I get this idea and I can’t shake it—like some customers are coming in to check on me, to see if I’m going to miss payments, because they keep asking about the new showroom. And I know my husband says they’re just being friendly, people ask about renovations, but it starts feeling like there’s another reason behind it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by an outside force, like someone is putting thoughts in your head? Or that your thoughts are being removed, or that others can hear your thoughts as you think them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in that way. I mean, I get muddled sometimes and I overthink things, especially when people are asking me too many questions, but I wouldn’t say it feels like someone is putting thoughts into my head or pulling them out. And I don’t really think people can hear what I’m thinking… though sometimes I do wonder if I’m giving something away with my face. You know, my mother used to say I had “no poker face,” which is funny because in jewelry you really do need one sometimes. But no, not like my thoughts are being broadcast or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had the experience of knowing, without the use of your usual senses, that something is happening or that someone is thinking something? For example, have you ever felt that someone else could know your thoughts, or that your thoughts were being taken out of your head or put in by someone else?\n\nPatient: Not like… hearing someone’s thoughts, no. I don’t think I can know things like that. But I do get convinced sometimes that people are coming into the shop for a reason they’re not saying. Like with the new showroom—customers ask all these questions, and after a while it feels like they’re checking whether I’m going to miss payments or whether something’s wrong. My husband says they’re just being friendly, and maybe they are, but in the moment I can’t shake it. It sounds odd when I say it out loud.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you—like something on TV, a song playing, or something someone says—have a special meaning that’s directed at you personally?\n\nPatient: Not really with TV or songs, I don’t think. It’s more… things people say in the shop. Like when customers keep asking about the new showroom—how it’s coming along, when it’ll be finished, all these little questions—and I start feeling like it means something more, like they’re checking whether I’m going to miss payments. My husband says they’re just being friendly, you know, people like to talk about renovations… and I know that sounds reasonable, but sometimes I get stuck on it and I can’t shake it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had the feeling that your thoughts are not your own, that someone else is responsible for them, or that you are losing control over your thoughts in some way?\n\nPatient: I don’t feel like someone else is putting them there, no… but I do feel like I lose the thread of them. That’s actually part of why I came in. Someone can ask me something simple at the shop, like when a ring repair will be ready, and I start answering, and suddenly I’m talking about invoices, then the soil I need for the garden, then mortgage papers, and I can see their face change like they don’t know how we got there. Then I get embarrassed and try to pull it back, but it feels like my thoughts kind of run off without me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, taken out, or that others around you can hear your thoughts as you think them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s like someone putting thoughts in my head, or people hearing them exactly. It’s more that I get stuck on an idea and it feels true at the time. Like with customers at the shop asking about the new showroom—too many questions, you know, when it’ll be done, how it’s going—and I start thinking they’re really there to see if I’m going to miss payments or if something’s wrong. My husband says they’re just being friendly, and maybe he’s right, but in the moment I get convinced. It sounds odd when I say it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, taken out of your mind, or that others around you can hear your thoughts as you think them?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. Not like someone is putting thoughts in, or taking them out, or hearing them. I mean, I can get muddled trying to explain it—like even now I’m thinking about whether I locked the display case before I left, which is silly—but that’s just me getting sidetracked. I haven’t noticed anything like my thoughts being controlled or broadcast.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe what it feels like when your thoughts seem to run off without you? Do they feel disconnected from each other, or do you feel like you're following a logical path even if others don't see the connection?\n\nPatient: It feels like I’m following it, sort of, while I’m saying it. Like there’s a connection in my head, but then when it comes out loud it’s… too many side roads. Someone asks me something simple, like when a ring repair will be ready, and I start to answer, but then I’m talking about the invoice for it, and that reminds me of the papers on my desk, and then somehow I’m thinking about garden soil I meant to buy, and then the mortgage papers, and by then I can see their face, like they’ve completely lost me. Then I realize I’ve gone too far off, but it’s hard to get back to the original question.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being put into your mind by an outside force, like someone is placing thoughts in your head, or that your thoughts are being taken out of your mind or that others can hear your thoughts as you're thinking them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think that’s happened, no. I know I keep circling around it, but it doesn’t feel like someone outside me is placing thoughts there or pulling them out. And I don’t have the sense that people can hear them while I’m thinking them.\n\nSometimes I lose my train of thought and then I’m thinking about three other things, like something at the shop or whether I left a drawer open, but that still feels like my own mind being busy, not like someone else is doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had the experience where it felt like your thoughts were being put into your mind by someone or something else, taken out of your mind, or that others around you could hear your thoughts as you were thinking them?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think so. I mean, I’ve had times where my thoughts jump around and I’m not finishing one before another one comes in, like if I’m looking at a stone setting and then suddenly remembering something completely different I meant to do… but it doesn’t feel like someone else put it there.\n\nAnd I haven’t noticed thoughts being taken out, or people hearing them. If someone guesses what I’m thinking, I usually figure it’s because my face gave it away or I said half of it out loud without realizing. But no, not in the way you’re asking, I don’t think.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone or something else, like they’re not coming from you at all?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, thoughts come in fast sometimes, like I’ll be thinking about one thing and then another thing just barges in, but it still feels like it’s from me. Not like someone planted it there.\n\nIt’s more like when I’m sorting stones and I mean to stay with one tray, and then I notice a different setting that needs cleaning, and then I’m halfway across the bench before I remember what I started doing. But that’s me getting pulled off track, not something outside putting thoughts in my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain events, actions, or objects in your environment were specifically directed at you or had a special meaning for you that others might not understand?\n\nPatient: Yes… sometimes. I know it probably sounds strange, but at the shop I’ve gotten it in my head that certain customers aren’t really there just to look at jewelry.\n\nLike they’ll ask a lot of questions about the new showroom, and I start thinking they’re checking whether I’m keeping up with payments or if I’m going to slip up somehow. But when they keep asking, it feels pointed at me, like there’s a reason behind it.\n\nAnd then I’ll start watching how they look at the cases, or if they glance at the counter too long… I don’t know.
